## P4 - `wait_write_config` silently treats an unmatched `wait` string as success

**Location:** [extreme/exos/docker/launch.py:67-93](https://github.com/srl-labs/vrnetlab/blob/d443cb61ba912648a917b106d6d600cba2dee811/extreme/exos/docker/launch.py#L67-L93)

**Problem:** `_Console.read_until` in [`common/vrnetlab.py`](https://github.com/srl-labs/vrnetlab/blob/d443cb61ba912648a917b106d6d600cba2dee811/common/vrnetlab.py#L1-L1) never raises on timeout; it breaks and returns the partial buffer. In `wait_write_config` the `except` branch therefore never catches a timeout, and the success `return` is reached even when the `wait` string never appeared, with no log. This logic is pre-existing, but this PR materially increases reliance on it&#58; the admin-password exchange now routes the `Current user's password:`, `New password:`, and `Reenter password:` steps through this function, and `bootstrap_spin` sets `self.running = True` regardless of whether any step matched. The `except` warning text &#40;&#34;Timeout waiting for prompt&#34;&#41; is also misleading because it only fires for genuine I/O errors.

**Trigger:** Any EXOS image whose prompt wording differs from the `wait` strings &#40;for example different capitalization&#41;, or a race in which the prompt text is consumed by an earlier read. The flow desyncs silently, the admin password is not set as intended, and SSH login with the configured credentials fails while the node is already marked running.

**Impact:** A broken bootstrap that is hard to diagnose because nothing is logged; the node reports as up but is unreachable over SSH with the provisioned credentials. No data loss or security exposure.

**Suggested correction:** After `read_until` returns, only `return` when the `wait` string was actually seen &#40;for example `wait.encode() in res`&#41;, and log a warning or take the retry path otherwise. Optional robustness improvement; the happy path the PR targets appears correct, so it does not block merge.


## Coverage

- The PR changes exactly one path, [`extreme/exos/docker/launch.py`](https://github.com/srl-labs/vrnetlab/blob/d443cb61ba912648a917b106d6d600cba2dee811/extreme/exos/docker/launch.py#L1-L1) &#40;modified&#41;; both head and base revisions were read in full and the diff was verified line by line against both.
- Relevant helpers were traced in [`common/vrnetlab.py`](https://github.com/srl-labs/vrnetlab/blob/d443cb61ba912648a917b106d6d600cba2dee811/common/vrnetlab.py#L1-L1)&#58; `_Console.read_until`, `_Console.read_very_eager`, `_Console.expect`, and `VM.wait_write`, plus the `bootstrap_spin` and `startup_config` callers of `wait_write_config`.
- Source-only review&#58; no tests were executed and none exist for `extreme/exos` &#40;`ale/vaos` and `fortinet` have test suites, but nothing covers this file or `wait_write_config`&#41;. The smallest useful check would be a unit test feeding a fake console to `wait_write_config` and asserting that a timed-out `read_until` is not reported as success.
- Could not verify offline that `configure ssh2 key algorithm rsa-sha2-512` is valid across the versions listed as tested in [`extreme/exos/README.md`](https://github.com/srl-labs/vrnetlab/blob/d443cb61ba912648a917b106d6d600cba2dee811/extreme/exos/README.md#L1-L1) &#40;32.6.3.126, 32.7.2.19, 33.6.1.14&#41;. Because `wait_write_config` does not detect command errors &#40;the `#` prompt returns either way&#41;, an unsupported command would fail silently. If confirmed on the tested images, no action is needed; otherwise a trace-level check of the response would surface it.
- Considered whether the retry logic could resend `configure account admin password` while the switch sits at a password prompt; this requires the phrase `configuration load` to appear in a response produced at an interactive prompt, which the CLI does not do, so it was not reported as a defect.
- No documentation or schema updates are required&#58; the change is internal bootstrap behavior of this node image, the EXOS README does not document bootstrap internals, and no topology schema is involved.
- Snapshot incompleteness per manifest&#58; two binary `OVMF.fd` files skipped &#40;irrelevant to this change&#41; and the repository rule documents `.cursor/rules/karpathy-guidelines.mdc` and `.cursor/rules/ponytail.mdc` were unavailable, so the supplied Karpathy and Ponytail criteria were applied from the task instructions without those files.

41: class EXOS_vm(vrnetlab.VM):
42:     def __init__(self, username, password, hostname, conn_mode):
43:         disk_image = None
44:         for e in sorted(os.listdir("/")):
45:             if not disk_image and re.search(".qcow2$", e):
46:                 disk_image = "/" + e

58:         self.num_nics = 13
59:         self.nic_type = "rtl8139"

61:     def wait_write_config(self, cmd, wait="#", retries=30, delay=5):
62:         """Execute command with retry logic for 'configuration load' state.
63:         
64:         EXOS can enter a 'configuration load' state during boot where config
65:         commands are rejected. This wrapper retries the command until success.
66:         """
67:         for attempt in range(retries):
68:             # drop any stale output (e.g. leftover prompts) so that read_until
69:             # below matches the prompt following *this* command
70:             self.tn.read_very_eager()
71:             self.tn.write(f"{cmd}\r".encode())
72:             try:
73:                 res = self.tn.read_until(wait.encode(), timeout=30)
74:                 response_str = res.decode(errors="ignore")
75:                 
76:                 # Check if we got the config load error
77:                 if "configuration load" in response_str.lower():
78:                     if attempt < retries - 1:  # Don't log on last attempt
79:                         self.logger.info(
80:                             f"'configuration load' detected, retrying '{cmd}' in {delay}s..."
81:                         )
82:                     time.sleep(delay)
83:                     continue
84:                     
85:                 # Success - command completed
86:                 return
87:             except Exception as e:
88:                 self.logger.warning(f"Timeout waiting for prompt after '{cmd}': {e}")
89:                 if attempt < retries - 1:
90:                     time.sleep(delay)
91:                 continue
92:                 
93:         self.logger.error(f"Command '{cmd}' failed after {retries} retries")
94: 
95:     def bootstrap_spin(self):
96:         """ This function should be called periodically to do work.
97:         """
98: 
99:         if self.spins > 300:
100:             # too many spins with no result ->  give up
101:             self.stop()
102:             self.start()
103:             return
104: 
105:         (ridx, match, res) = self.tn.expect(
106:             [rb'node is now available for login.', rb'\[[yY]\/[nN]\/q\]'], 1
107:         )
108: 
109:         if match:  # got a match!
110:             if ridx == 0:
111:                 time.sleep(1)
112:                 self.wait_write(cmd="", wait=None)
113:                 self.wait_write(cmd="admin", wait="login:")
114:                 self.wait_write(cmd="", wait="password:")
115:             else:
116:                 self.wait_write(cmd="q", wait=None)
117:                 self.wait_write(cmd="", wait="#")
118:                 self.logger.info("Found config prompt")
119:                 # run main config!
120:                 self.logger.info("Running bootstrap_config()")
121:                 self.bootstrap_config()
122:                 self.startup_config()
123:                 time.sleep(1)
124:                 # close telnet connection
125:                 self.tn.close()
126:                 # startup time?
127:                 startup_time = datetime.datetime.now() - self.start_time
128:                 self.logger.info("Startup complete in: %s" % startup_time)
129:                 # mark as running
130:                 self.running = True
131:                 return
132: 
133:         # no match, if we saw some output from the router it's probably
134:         # booting, so let's give it some more time
135:         if res != b"":
136:             self.logger.trace("OUTPUT: %s" % res.decode())
137:             # reset spins if we saw some output
138:             self.spins = 0
139: 
140:         self.spins += 1
141: 
142:         return
143: 
144:     def bootstrap_config(self):
145:         """ Do the actual bootstrap config
146:         """
147:         self.wait_write_config(cmd=f"configure snmp sysName {self.hostname}")
148:         self.wait_write_config(cmd="unconfigure vlan Mgmt ipaddress")
149:         self.wait_write_config(cmd=f"configure vlan Mgmt ipaddress {self.mgmt_address_ipv4}")
150:         self.wait_write_config(cmd=f"configure iproute add default {self.mgmt_gw_ipv4} vr VR-Mgmt")
151:         if self.username == "admin":
152:             self.wait_write_config(
153:                 cmd="configure account admin password", wait="Current user's password:"
154:             )
155:             self.wait_write_config(cmd="", wait="New password:")
156:             self.wait_write_config(cmd=self.password, wait="Reenter password:")
157:             self.wait_write_config(cmd=self.password)
158:         else:
159:             self.wait_write_config(
160:                 cmd=f"create account admin {self.username} {self.password}"
161:             )
162:         self.wait_write_config(cmd="disable cli prompting")
163:         self.wait_write_config(cmd="configure ssh2 key")
164:         # sign with SHA-2 instead of SHA-1 (ssh-rsa is rejected by recent clients,
165:         # e.g. paramiko 5); must be set before ssh2 is enabled to avoid a restart
166:         self.wait_write_config(cmd="configure ssh2 key algorithm rsa-sha2-512")
167:         self.wait_write_config(cmd="enable ssh2")
168:         self.wait_write_config(cmd="save")
169: 
170:     def startup_config(self):
171:         if not os.path.exists(STARTUP_CONFIG_FILE):
172:             self.logger.trace(f"Startup config file {STARTUP_CONFIG_FILE} not found")
173:             self.wait_write_config(cmd="enable cli prompting")
174:             return
175:         if not self.mgmt_passthrough:
176:             # qemu user-mode network serves /tftpboot on 10.0.0.2
177:             vrnetlab.run_command(["cp", STARTUP_CONFIG_FILE, "/tftpboot/containerlab.xsf"])
178:             self.wait_write_config(cmd="tftp get 10.0.0.2 vr VR-Mgmt containerlab.xsf")
179:             self.wait_write_config(cmd="load script containerlab.xsf")
180:         else:
181:             # Transparent mgmt: no TFTP server reachable, push the config over
182:             # the serial console instead (cli prompting is still disabled here).
183:             with open(STARTUP_CONFIG_FILE) as file:
184:                 config_lines = [line.rstrip() for line in file.readlines()]
185: 
186:             self.logger.info(f"Writing lines from {STARTUP_CONFIG_FILE}")
187:             for line in config_lines:
188:                 if not line or line.startswith("#"):
189:                     continue
190:                 self.wait_write_config(cmd=line)
191:         self.wait_write_config(cmd="save")
192:         self.wait_write_config(cmd="enable cli prompting")
193: 
194: 
195: class EXOS(vrnetlab.VR):
196:     def __init__(self, hostname, username, password, conn_mode):
197:         super(EXOS, self).__init__(username, password)
198:         self.vms = [EXOS_vm(username, password, hostname, conn_mode)]

This wrapper retries the command until success.\n        \"\"\"\n        for attempt in range(retries):\n            # drop any stale output (e.g. leftover prompts) so that read_until\n            # below matches the prompt following *this* command\n            self.tn.read_very_eager()\n            self.tn.write(f\"{cmd}\\r\".encode())\n            try:\n                res = self.tn.read_until(wait.encode(), timeout=30)\n                response_str = res.decode(errors=\"ignore\")\n                \n                # Check if we got the config load error\n                if \"configuration load\" in response_str.lower():\n                    if attempt < retries - 1:  # Don't log on last attempt\n                        self.logger.info(\n                            f\"'configuration load' detected, retrying '{cmd}' in {delay}s...\"\n                        )\n                    time.sleep(delay)\n                    continue\n                    \n                # too many spins with no result ->  give up\n            self.stop()\n            self.start()\n            return\n\n        (ridx, match, res) = self.tn.expect(\n            [rb'node is now available for login.', rb'\\[[yY]\\/[nN]\\/q\\]'], 1\n        )\n\n        if match:  # got a match!\n            if ridx == 0:\n                time.sleep(1)\n                self.wait_write(cmd=\"\", wait=None)\n                self.wait_write(cmd=\"admin\", wait=\"login:\")\n                self.wait_write(cmd=\"\", wait=\"password:\")\n            else:\n                self.wait_write(cmd=\"q\", wait=None)\n                self.wait_write(cmd=\"\", wait=\"#\")\n                self.logger.info(\"Found config prompt\")\n                # run main config!\n                self.logger.info(\"Running bootstrap_config()\")\n                self.bootstrap_config()\n                self.startup_config()\n                time.sleep(1)\n                # close telnet connection\n                self.tn.close()\n                # startup time?\n                startup_time = datetime.datetime.now() - self.start_time\n                self.logger.info(\"Startup complete in: %s\" % startup_time)\n                # mark as running\n                self.running = True\n                return\n\n        # no match, if we saw some output from the router it's probably\n        # booting, so let's give it some more time\n        if res != b\"\":\n            self.logger.trace(\"OUTPUT: %s\" % res.decode())\n            # reset spins if we saw some output\n            self.spins = 0\n\n        self.spins += 1\n\n        return\n\n    def bootstrap_config(self):\n        \"\"\" Do the actual bootstrap config\n        \"\"\"\n        self.wait_write_config(cmd=f\"configure snmp sysName {self.hostname}\")\n        self.wait_write_config(cmd=\"unconfigure vlan Mgmt ipaddress\")\n        self.wait_write_config(cmd=f\"configure vlan Mgmt ipaddress {self.mgmt_address_ipv4}\")\n        self.wait_write_config(cmd=f\"configure iproute add default {self.mgmt_gw_ipv4} vr VR-Mgmt\")\n        if self.username == \"admin\":\n            self.wait_write_config(\n                cmd=\"configure account admin password\", wait=\"Current user's password:\"\n            )\n            self.wait_write_config(cmd=\"\", wait=\"New password:\")\n            self.wait_write_config(cmd=self.password, wait=\"Reenter password:\")\n            self.wait_write_config(cmd=self.password)\n        else:\n            self.wait_write_config(\n                cmd=f\"create account admin {self.username} {self.password}\"\n            )\n        self.wait_write_config(cmd=\"disable cli prompting\")\n        self.wait_write_config(cmd=\"configure ssh2 key\")\n        # sign with SHA-2 instead of SHA-1 (ssh-rsa is rejected by recent clients,\n        # e.g. paramiko 5); must be set before ssh2 is enabled to avoid a restart\n        self.wait_write_config(cmd=\"configure ssh2 key algorithm rsa-sha2-512\")\n        self.wait_write_config(cmd=\"enable ssh2\")\n        self.wait_write_config(cmd=\"save\")\n\n    def startup_config(self):\n        if not os.path.exists(STARTUP_CONFIG_FILE):\n            self.logger.trace(f\"Startup config file {STARTUP_CONFIG_FILE} not found\")\n            self.wait_write_config(cmd=\"enable cli prompting\")\n            return\n        if not self.mgmt_passthrough:\n            # qemu user-mode network serves /tftpboot on 10.0.0.2\n            vrnetlab.run_command([\"cp\", STARTUP_CONFIG_FILE, \"/tftpboot/containerlab.xsf\"])\n            self.wait_write_config(cmd=\"tftp get 10.0.0.2 vr VR-Mgmt containerlab.xsf\")\n            self.wait_write_config(cmd=\"load script containerlab.xsf\")\n        else:\n            # 2. More important: `wait_write_config` flushes stale output with `read_very_eager()` before writing each command. 4. Potential real issue with the retry loop and prompts: After `read_until(wait, 30)` returns because it found the wait string, the code checks "configuration load" in response. If the response contains the wait string, success. But what if the "configuration load" error message comes AFTER the wait string in the same buffer? E.g., wait="#" and the command output includes "... configuration load ..." then "#"? Then the code retries even though the command succeeded — resending `configure account admin password` would restart the password prompt sequence. Edge case.

I could flag as unverifiable, but that's speculative — better to put in coverage notes.

Another angle: `configure ssh2 key algorithm rsa-sha2-512` — on older EXOS versions this command may not exist, and with the retry logic, an unknown command returns to "#" prompt, so wait matches and the loop just logs error? Actually wait_write_config doesn't check for command errors at all; it just waits for the wait string. If the command errors ("Error: ..."), the "#" prompt still appears, so it proceeds. Fine — but then on EXOS versions lacking the command, the feature silently doesn't apply (and ssh-rsa remains). Not blocking; it's a version-compat question worth noting in coverage.

Now, the bigger thing to check: is there an existing helper in vrnetlab.py for this pattern (wait_write with clean_buffer)? `wait_write` has a `clean_buffer` param that calls `con.read_very_eager()` AFTER reading the wait string. The new code's flush is BEFORE writing. The simplest approach might have been to use `wait_write(..., clean_buffer=True)` which clears remaining chars after the wait — that achieves the same effect (clearing output that follows the matched prompt before the next write). Indeed `wait_write(clean_buffer=True)` reads very eager after read_until — dropping the residual output between the matched prompt and next command. The new wait_write_config does flush before write, which is equivalent in effect. But wait_write doesn't have the "configuration load" retry logic, which is why wait_write_config exists. So not a strong duplication finding. Hmm, but one could argue wait_write_config could reuse... no, they're different. Skip.

Now potential correctness issue in the new flush: `read_very_eager()` output is discarded without logging. The `_Console.read_very_eager` mirrors data to stdout, so it still appears in docker logs. Fine.

A REAL issue: In `wait_write_config`, the new flush also discards the output of the PREVIOUS command beyond its matched prompt. Consider the "disable cli prompting" command: after it runs, prompting is disabled. Fine.

But consider the flush interacting with password prompts: After `wait_write_config(cmd="configure account admin password", wait="Current user's password:")` — read_until returns as soon as "Current user's password:" is seen. The remainder (nothing usually). Next call flushes. OK.

Now — the one potentially significant behavioral regression: with the flush before EVERY write, in the password sequence, `wait_write_config(cmd="", wait="New password:")`: flush occurs, then empty line sent. But here's the catch: does EXOS prompt "Current user's password:" immediately? If yes, empty line = current password (empty, valid on fresh boot since admin has no password initially). Then "New password:" appears. OK.

But WAIT — there's an important detail: the old code's flow ended after "Reenter password:" without waiting for "#" — the new code's last call waits for "#" with retries. If during password change the switch is in "configuration load" state... irrelevant.

Another thing: `wait_write_config` retry semantics with the password prompt sequence: if "configuration load" appears in response while waiting for "Current user's password:", the retry resends the whole command. But now the switch may be sitting AT the "Current user's password:" prompt; resending `configure account admin password\r` at that prompt would be treated as the current password input! Hmm: retry: flush stale output (drops the prompt possibly), write command again. The switch at "Current user's password:" prompt receives "configure account admin password\r" as password input — wrong, and then "New password:" appears with garbage... Desync. But this only triggers in the rare "configuration load" race. Pre-existing behavior for wait_write_config's other commands? Previously, the account commands used wait_write (no retry), so the retry didn't apply to them. Now the password sequence is subject to retry logic, which can desync the multi-prompt exchange if "configuration load" is detected mid-sequence. Severity: P3/P4 — edge case. Hmm, actually can "configuration load" happen during bootstrap_config? The whole point of wait_write_config is EXOS enters config load state during boot; bootstrap_config runs right after boot. So possible. But the retry logic applies per-command; for a multi-prompt interactive command, retrying the top-level command from mid-sequence is problematic. Worth a P3/P4 note.

Now let me also check: does `wait_write_config(cmd="", wait="New password:")` risk matching "#"? No, it waits for the literal "New password:".

Big potential problem #1: If EXOS prompts "Current user's password:" immediately after the command (likely), the old code was broken-ish; new code seems more coherent. But if EXOS requires an extra Enter (as old code assumed with wait="#"), the new code's first wait for "Current user's password:" would still see it (since EXOS prints it regardless of extra enter? If an enter is needed, then "Current user's password:" never appears until an enter is sent... then wait times out after 30s and wait_write_config returns (read_until returns buf without the wait string; "configuration load" not in it → success!). So wait_write_config treats timeout as SUCCESS. That's a pre-existing flaw in wait_write_config (read_until doesn't raise; the except never fires; timeout → return "success"). This is important: the `except Exception` branch is dead code for timeouts; on timeout, read_until returns partial buf, and the function returns success silently. The PR's retry logic therefore doesn't actually retry on prompt timeout at all — only when "configuration load" text is present. And after retries exhausted, just logs error.

Wait, is that pre-existing? Yes, wait_write_config's timeout-as-success flaw predates this PR (the function with the same logic is in base too, minus the flush). The PR didn't introduce it. But the PR builds on this function and expands its use to the password exchange. The relevant new aspect: if the wait for "Current user's password:" times out (wrong assumption about needing an extra Enter), the code proceeds silently and the password change desyncs, leaving admin password unchanged → subsequent SSH auth with the configured password fails. Old code with wait_write had timeout=None → wait="#" would block forever → hang (container never becomes healthy). New code silently desyncs instead of hanging. Either is bad; new code fails "faster" but silently.

# Code Review: EXOS bootstrap password flow and SSH key algorithm fix

## Scope

The PR modifies a single file, `extreme/exos/docker/launch.py`, with three related changes:

1. A `read_very_eager()` flush at the top of each `wait_write_config` retry iteration, so `read_until` matches the prompt belonging to the command just written instead of stale output from a previous exchange.
2. The admin-password exchange is moved from `wait_write` (with `wait="#"` on the first call) to `wait_write_config` with prompt-accurate wait strings (`Current user's password:`, `New password:`, `Reenter password:`).
3. A new `configure ssh2 key algorithm rsa-sha2-512` command is issued before `enable ssh2`, with a comment explaining that SHA-2 signatures are needed because recent clients reject `ssh-rsa`.

I traced the change against `_Console` (`read_until`, `read_very_eager`, `expect`) and `wait_write` in `common/vrnetlab.py`, the `bootstrap_spin` caller, and the `startup_config` consumer of `wait_write_config`.

## Assessment

The core change is a genuine improvement. Under the `_Console` wrapper in `common/vrnetlab.py`, `read_until` with `timeout=None` (the `wait_write` default) blocks indefinitely until the pattern is seen, so the base revision's `wait_write(cmd="configure account admin password", wait="#")` would hang forever if EXOS responds with `Current user's password:` instead of a `#` prompt. Waiting for the actual prompts bounds each step and matches the real EXOS dialog. The flush is also sound: `_Console.read_very_eager()` polls with `select` and never blocks, so the pre-write flush is cheap and safe.

One summary table, then details.

| Priority | Count | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| P0 | 0 | — |
| P1 | 0 | — |
| P2 | 0 | — |
| P3 | 0 | — |
| P4 | 1 | `wait_write_config` silently treats an unmatched wait string as success; the new password flow leans on this |

## Finding detail

### P4 — `wait_write_config` declares success when the expected prompt never appears

**Location:** `extreme/exos/docker/launch.py` lines 67–93, head revision ([permalink](https://github.com/srl-labs/vrnetlab/blob/d443cb61ba912648a917b106d6d600cba2dee811/extreme/exos/docker/launch.py#L67-L93)).

**Problem:** `_Console.read_until` in `common/vrnetlab.py` (lines 156–169) never raises on timeout; it breaks out of the loop and returns whatever partial buffer it accumulated. In `wait_write_config`, the `try`/`except` therefore never catches a timeout, and the success path (`return` after the `configuration load` check) is reached even when `wait` never appeared in `response_str`. A timeout produces no log at all, while the `except` branch — whose message says "Timeout waiting for prompt" — only fires for genuine I/O errors, making that warning misleading. This logic is pre-existing in the base revision, but this PR materially increases reliance on it: the admin-password exchange now routes three prompt-sensitive steps (`Current user's password:`, `New password:`, `Reenter password:`) through this function, and `bootstrap_config` completes with `self.running = True` in `bootstrap_spin` regardless of whether any step actually matched.

**Trigger:** Any EXOS image or version whose prompt wording differs (for example different capitalization of the password prompts), or a race in which the prompt text is consumed by an earlier read. The flow then desyncs silently, the password is not set as intended, and SSH login with the configured credentials fails while the node is already marked running.

**Impact:** A broken bootstrap that is hard to diagnose because nothing is logged; the node reports as up but is unreachable over SSH with the provisioned credentials. No data loss or security exposure.

**Correction:** After `read_until` returns, verify the wait string was actually seen, e.g. only `return` when `wait.encode() in res`, and log a warning (or take the retry path) when it was not. This is an optional robustness improvement — the happy path the PR targets appears correct, so it does not block merge.

## Coverage notes

- Only one path changed (`extreme/exos/docker/launch.py`, status `M`); both revisions were read in full and the diff was verified against both. The manifest reports the snapshot is incomplete only because two binary `OVMF.fd` files were skipped (irrelevant to this change) and because the repository rule documents `.cursor/rules/karpathy-guidelines.mdc` and `.cursor/rules/ponytail.mdc` were unavailable — the review criteria from the supplied rules were applied without those files.
- This was a source-only review; no tests were executed and none exist for `extreme/exos` (`ale/vaos` and `fortinet` have test suites, but nothing covers this file or `wait_write_config`). The smallest useful check would be a unit test that feeds a fake console to `wait_write_config` and asserts that a timed-out `read_until` is not reported as success.
- I could not verify offline that `configure ssh2 key algorithm rsa-sha2-512` is valid across the versions listed as tested in `extreme/exos/README.md` (32.6.3.126, 32.7.2.19, 33.6.1.14). Since `wait_write_config` does not detect command errors (the `#` prompt returns either way), an unsupported command would fail silently and the stated SHA-2 goal would not be achieved on that version. If the author confirmed the command on the tested images, no action is needed; otherwise, a trace-level check of the response would surface it.
- I considered whether the retry logic could resend `configure account admin password` while the switch sits at a password prompt (desyncing the exchange). This requires the word `configuration load` to appear in a response produced while the switch is at an interactive prompt, which the CLI does not do, so I did not report it as a defect.
- No documentation or schema updates are required: the change is internal bootstrap behavior of this node image, the EXOS README does not document bootstrap internals, and no topology schema is involved.
